ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e2678	Federal Chancellor! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XXI/M/M_00116/index.shtml -- 116/M]] In view of the economic crisis which it has caused, why does the Federal Government not take action against the high level of unemployment?
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e2706	Please, Mr. Federal Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e2721	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Of course, the expression "the economic crisis which it is contributing to" is a joke, because you know very well that there are now rather gloomy economic prospects around the world. Or do you think that the red and green government is not responsible for the international economic crisis or that the Americans and the Japanese have nothing to do with it? [[Abg. Edlinger: Not to the Austrian voters!]] So I would recommend – also and especially you as an economic expert in the Chamber of Labour – that we deal with the international forecasts a little bit. Of these, it's quite clear before the 11th. September, but especially since then, the trend towards a significantly weaker economic development is evident. We have seen this coming and, with the help of a number of key experts, the economic researchers, the central bank, the government debt committee, the social partners, we have had several talks to counter this in time. This is reflected in the comparison with the Federal Republic of Germany: We have addressed our budget problems considerably more courageously and have gotten under control more quickly, but we still have an increase of 0.6 percent above our most important and largest trading partner! This is certainly a sign of a good economic policy. There are now around 31,000 people more in employment in Austria than in October 1999. So economic policy works. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] The labour market service is reformed, which is reflected in the average length of stay in unemployment. Two years ago, the average duration of unemployment was 133 days, today it is 104 days – a great success for Martin Bartenstein and the AMS team! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We have also deepened integration – and I say this clearly about it. Today, the integration decree allows 14 000 foreigners who are legally resident in Austria to work here, which, of course, has put some pressure on the labour market, but I still believe that it is right that people who live here can also work. The 11th. September has brought drastic intensifications, for example, in aviation. We helped with a state guarantee. We have helped those workers who have difficulties and are at risk of termination through special training and training facilities in the field of labour market services, and we will continue along this successful path.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e4162	Federal Chancellor! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XXI/M/M_00112/index.shtml -- 112/M]] After 30 years of discussion, what improvements does the administrative reform now bring to citizens between the Federal Government and the countries?
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e4189	Please, Mr. Federal Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e4205	Firstly, after this administrative reform, the citizen will know that his point of contact, his centre, is the county headquarters. Secondly, it can already insist that it will be used differently with the new electronic possibilities and that it will have quite different possibilities in communication with its authority – as of 2003 for example, there will be the electronic tax declaration, we will be introducing the electronic legislative procedure next year, so that we can then inform ourselves about assessments and their intermediaries via the Internet – and the mandatory procedural concentration at the BH will result in the fact that, in particular, the smaller and medium-sized enterprises no longer need to "go back and forth" but that the procedures are really concentrated. In addition, we also work on certain other reliefs: multi-year funding commitments, for example in the cultural field – this helps artists, gives them legal certainty – the extension of assessment periods, the re-regulation of assessment for new motor vehicles will save the vehicle owner, all Austrians, about 200 million shillings per year; the procedure before the agricultural Senates should be clearly simplified, which will, of course, help farmers; the numerous authorisation obligations that exist today in the healthcare sector should be simplified and made simple reporting obligations, and this will, of course, help those who practice such a profession. For example, the very much debated combination of district courts also has the essential advantage that it will not give the citizen a service in a small court, a district court, for one or two days, but a full service. Similarly, the reform of the Gendarmerie Posts will simply make more people on the road for safety and not for administrations. The same applies to the school administration: The number of teachers working in the administration is reduced by 10 percent, which means that more teachers can be active for the children for whom young people can be pedagogical. I think everyone will have something of it!

As regards the post: Do not be angry with me, honourable Member, if, for example, in a rural region of the last Greißler, the last merchant or a hostess can take away various things in the sense of a full service, instead of having individual non-lifeable locations, which are then overall very expensive and which the citizen ultimately has to finance with his tax money – there is our way of modernizing!

ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e5263	Please, Mr. Federal Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e5279	Deputies! We have a joint declaration of government that sets out our objectives. And it is true today – this is actually a very interesting course that has now taken place – that six Member States, namely Greece, Ireland, Denmark, Portugal, Luxembourg and Austria, have never entered the civil production of nuclear power and Italy has already gone out. Eight Member States of the Union still have nuclear power plants today, but five of them have already decided to exit, namely Germany, Belgium, Sweden, Spain and the Netherlands. Three countries stick to this: United Kingdom, France and Finland. It is clear that such an exit is, of course, only possible voluntarily, because it is a principle – and we have also put great value on it – that each country makes its decisions autonomously here. But the interesting thing is: The trend is going in the right direction. Germany, for example, decided in June 2001, together with leading nuclear power producers, to end the use of nuclear power in Germany; the standard duration of the nuclear power plants is limited to 32 years. Belgium announced its exit in July 1999 and the period is to be extended to 40 years. I think you can and should continue on this path. The Austrian voice, which always strongly pleads for a total exit, will not be muted.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e8579	Mr President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! Minister! Mr Finanzminister! Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that this is not the purpose and purpose of today's debate. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Outsourcings and privatisations are on the agenda of modern states. – This is in line with the basic position of the People's Party: What private can do, the state should leave it. This does not mean, however, that the state should and can withdraw from all areas. The principle: "More private, less state!", just indicate the direction in which we want to develop our community. Certain areas are and should be reserved for the State, such as, of course, military defence. The Court of Auditors' report, which is under discussion, deals, inter alia, with the outsourcing of tasks in the Federal Economic Service. The performance review concerned essential areas, in a total of four sub-areas. First: Transport and storage tasks. The Court of Auditors praises the good cooperation of the German army with the ÖBB, but at the same time complains that no tender has been made. Nevertheless, I think it is important that, regardless of calls for proposals, cooperation between the ÖBB and the Federal Army is constantly taking place, so that these two organisations can work well together even in the case of use. In addition, the Court of Auditors recommends that their own vehicles be better used and that they be used to transport economic equipment. This allows the fixed cost share to be pressed and divided into several areas. To optimize this However, there is a need for an up-to-date overview of the fleet's operating schedules, and the Court of Auditors therefore encourages the creation of an IT-supported fleet. This procedure has already been initiated by the Federal Army. Secondly, In connection with the catering, the Federal Ministry points out that there is a high level of staff intensity that, for example, there is a high cost of renovation for some of its own kitchen facilities. It was considered whether this task cannot be outsourced, whether it cannot be passed on to outsourced companies. The problem in this area is that, of course, practice and use-specific catering tasks cannot be privatized. In addition, the renovation in the kitchen area cannot be done by private individuals at a zero rate. In other words, private operators also have to take into account these restructuring costs and cannot simply drop them under the table. I therefore do not think that outsourcing can lead to savings in this area. The third area concerns the supply of supplies where there is a great deal to be saved, first of all through a low military threat intensity and, second, by the possibility of obtaining all the goods on the market in the short term. This means less procurement costs and lower storage costs. In this area, it is possible to save without endangering security of supply. Finally, fourthly, a savings can be made in the clothing and equipment of our soldiers. This is possible through direct delivery by producers where the equipment and equipment are used. This allows intermediate storage to be saved or at least reduced. With this step, the army adapts to the logistics methods of the private economy. To sum up, I can say – and I believe this from the report – that the Ministry and the Court of Auditors are working very well, that on the one hand the Ministry is trying to save costs from itself, and on the other hand, the Court of Auditors is also concerned about how, despite the need for savings, the basic needs of the federal army are guaranteed. In principle, a meaningful balance is sought between domestic services and private awards. All in all, a good report from the Court of Auditors, positive signals from the ministry. Keep going!

ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e8927	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Federal Minister! Mr President of the Court of Auditors! High house! The Court of Auditors' Special Report on Banking Supervision focuses on Rieger Bank's criminal case. This crime has spread over the years 1981 to 1999. So it actually took almost 20 years to put an end to these fraudulent machinations of the owners and the bank auditors. Of course, they ask themselves: How long could it take for the various competent supervisory bodies to have intervened within the framework of banking supervision? It is interesting to read that, despite this long duration and the great damage of several billion schilling, which has finally arisen for banks and small investors, no failure of the institutions in the banking supervision was observed after this detailed audit from the Court’s point of view. On the other hand, the Court notes very well that there have been organisational shortcomings in banking supervision, that there have been problems in the enforcement of banking supervision, that the legal framework has been problematic and that there have been staff shortages. I can briefly tell you this complex system: In 1998, the Oesterreichische National Bank informed banking supervision of incorrect information on the issue of bonds. Banking supervision makes a statement of a case-law to the prosecutor's office, but the prosecutor's office does not enter because it sees no need to do so in itself. There is also a request from the bank supervision to the Federal Securities Supervisory Board. It only enters ten months later and then, despite all this, does not make an on-site test. . So you see that several authorities could have intervened in this very complex supervisory procedure. But that was the old model. Since July of this year, there is a new model, a new Financial Markets Supervisory Authority in the sense of an all-financial supervision that would improve many of these problems. For example, it provides for a better enforceability of measures by pooling the investigative authority and the administrative criminal authority, dealing at all with fewer bodies and applying stricter exclusion provisions to unqualified owners. There is also an improvement with regard to the bank auditor – the one at Rieger Bank – by having to be exchanged every six years in the future. In addition, the auditors should not be as high as they used to be in the auditing bank, so there is only a very minimal participation, and the supervision of the financial markets may also prohibit orders from auditors. These would be serious improvements, and I can understand President Fiedler's statement that the new legal situation of financial market supervision will improve the organisation, so that cases like Rieger Bank probably could not be avoided, but could possibly be detected earlier. - That's my interpretation. Ladies and gentlemen! I feel it is incredibly sad and inconvenient that this pan-financial supervision, this new financial market supervision, was not given the two-thirds majority by the Socialist Party, which it needed to ensure its constitutional basis. For I believe that this financial market supervision is an important element in order not to prevent events such as the Rieger Bank, but to a large extent to prevent them. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e10538	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! [[General condolences.]] I should like to thank you for this praise, but at the same time I would like to say that this praise, to a large extent, has to be applied to the auditors of the Rechnungshof, or to them, because, without their overriding work, the reports that have been discussed today are not available. [[General condolences.]] On the other hand, I should also like to refer to the Committee's work and discussions in the Committee, my personal thanks, but also to the Court of Auditors' thanks for the fact that there was genuine support for the Court of Auditors' concerns. I have also been given the impression in the very factual discussions that Parliamentarians, all political groups, are trying to maintain good cooperation with the Court of Auditors. I see this as a real milestone in democratic cooperation between the Court of Auditors and Parliament, just as the Federal Constitution presupposes, for the Court of Auditors acts as the body of the National Council and provides the National Council with the necessary information so that the National Council can exercise its budgetary sovereignty and all that is related to it. On the other side Therefore, cooperation with the National Council for the Court of Auditors is beneficial because it can be expected through the discussions in the Committee or in the plenary session – rightly expected! – that the consideration of the issues raised by the Court of Auditors at the same time contributes to making it easier to implement the recommendations made by the Court of Auditors in its reports. I can say that the discussions that have been held in the Committee have certainly contributed to this in connection with the 1999 activity report, for which I would also like to thank myself. We know that a number of recommendations in this report have already been implemented in the meantime, not least through the support given to us by the Court of Auditors. High house! However, I would also like to speak to something else and I would also like to make a request to do so. Over the past few years, the Court of Auditors has experienced a tremendous increase in its tasks: on the one hand, in the field of audit, such as the examinations of the Chambers and also the examinations relating to the funds of the European Union. In addition to his actual examination work, he has also obtained tasks of other kinds, such as income surveys or the very complex statistical surveys according to the Relevant Restriction Act. I do not want to speak in detail about the international tasks of the Court of Auditors which it is also necessary to carry out. It has also been used more and more in recent years for examinations which have been carried out by the National Council or by the parliamentary groups, as was the case, for example, with regard to the report on banking supervision which is also under consideration today. The Court of Auditors is very pleased to comply with these contracts, which are set out in the Constitution, but it should be borne in mind that its human resources are limited. He points out that these human resources also need to be filled up again and again. I have heard with great goodwill today that several Members have expressed very clearly that they have spoken against a reduction in the Court of Auditors' staff. I can only welcome this. I would like to ask all the political groups in this House to make this statement that it is not intended to reduce the level of staff of the Court of Auditors to take account of the next budget and the decision-making of these budgets, because the impact of the Court of Auditors and the quality of its work can only be maintained if it has the appropriate human resources and if it has no loss of resources. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the Greens]] I believe that it is also in the interest of this House that this impact of the Court of Auditors is preserved, because in the end it is also ensured that the flow of information from the Court of Auditors to you can be maintained in the same quantity and in the same quality and that the common basis for cooperation is also available in the future. Thank you.

It makes a lot of sense, therefore, that, if, now and for many years, 71 lakes, including tourist core areas such as the Wolfgangsee, the Grundlsee and other lakes, were managed by the Austrian Federal Forests – to the satisfaction of the country, to the satisfaction of the communities, to the satisfaction of tourism companies – now says: We are also giving the twelve other lakes into the hands of the Austrian Federal Forest as regards the administration and thus saving money in favour of the taxpayer. – This is the first essential point. The second point is that we have achieved through a modification of the seashore policy that pre-shore landings – i.e. seabeds – can buy these land areas belonging to the Republic. This brings in about 209 million schilling of revenue for the Republic. We have agreed with the country of Carinthia that these 209 million schillings will be used in consensus and on a proposal from the country of Carinthia for the purchase of marine property for the population in order to improve public access to the lakes. This means a significant improvement for the Carinthian population in terms of access to the lakes. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Ladies and gentlemen! [[Abg. Leikam: No polemics from the government bank!]] So far, the lakes are not subject to the constitutional protection of the substance, which is prescribed for the Austrian Federal Forest, but theoretically, it is possible to sell lakes by empowering a simple majority of this House. [[Abg. Leikam: (keeping) Today decided! Kärntner Landtag!]] With the decision we have taken, the lakes are also covered by constitutionally protected substance conservation. That is, there is a greater protection for our waters, a greater protection for our lakes than we ever had before. This really made it possible to achieve an increase in quality. Deputies! ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e14219	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Mr Finanzminister! Mr President of the Court of Auditors! The year 2000 represents a trend change in Austrian budget policy. For the first time in the history of government budgets, no less than three budgets were drawn up in one year. – I remember: There was a red finance minister who didn't bring it to a single budget! – The consolidation rate, which started in 2000, was deliberately defined in the medium term and is now being implemented quickly. Total expenditure was around 801 billion Schilling, compared with 762 billion Schilling revenue. This results in a drop of around 39 billion schilling. Thus, it was around 15 billion schilling or 42 percent smaller than forecast. This deficit reduction alone means savings of around 840 million schilling in interest rates. The fact that the current Federal Government's promise of saving itself has also been implemented proves that expenditure in the Chancellor's Office was reduced by 26 percent and even by 34 percent in financial management. For example, in the areas of transport, innovation and technology, additional expenditure of around 11 billion schilling was incurred in order to further secure the economic upturn at that time – a plus of 44 percent – in the areas of transport, innovation and technology. The good economy brought higher revenue to the government budget than expected. Budgetary measures and one-off effects had to offset the budgetary burden caused by the tax reform adopted in 1999. In the 1999 election, the tax reform was decided by the then Minister of Finance, but it was not provided for. This had to be achieved only by the new blue-black German government in 2000. Private households also strongly increased their consumption expenditure in 2000. The real increase in gross domestic product was 3.3 percent in 2000 and therefore averaged the EU average. Consumer prices rose by only 2.3 percent in the year under review. The development in the labour market was also very encouraging: If the unemployment rate was 6.7% in 1999, it fell to 5.8 percent in 2000. In summary, there was a very high level of employment, a sufficiently stable monetary development and a high level of external balance in 2000 and a secure growth potential. The year 2000 was also the year of privatisation: The PSK was sold around 18 billion schilling. In October 2000, the federal shares were sold at Vienna Airport and also the state printing companies. The highest privatisation revenues were achieved in the sale of Telekom and in the auction of UMTS licenses. All these amounts were not used to stop budget holes, as was the case in the previous years, but the debt of ÖIAG was reduced by more than 60 billion schilling. This useful use means that in future around 3 billion schillings will have to be paid less at interest rates each year. In mature economies and not least as a result of European structural change, the importance of banking and credit, capital markets, insurance and other financial services is constantly increasing. Since banking supervision – the Ministry of Finance is currently overseeing around 920 credit institutions – was more efficient in dealing with the crisis, the debate on their reorganisation began in 2000 at government level. The law establishing this important financial market surveillance authority was adopted by Parliament this year. The consolidation course taken in 2000 proved to be very correct and is an essential part of a healthy overall economic strategy. This budget strategy is light at the end of a decades-long debt tunnel, and the evidence of its accuracy and effectiveness is constantly presented by our Minister of Finance. Thank you!

ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e15076	Mr President. High house! I would first like to make a few basic remarks on this urgent question and then answer the individual questions. Ladies and gentlemen! Education is the most important prerequisite for the development of the economy in a country, education is the most important prerequisite for economic activity, education should therefore be in the central interest of each party, and education should be borne jointly by all parties. There has been this educational-popular desire and a Party-political advertising of SPÖs and numerous front-end organisations. These advertising materials have been used to distribute numerous statements by SPÖ politicians, including the President of the Federal Party, Gusenbauer. It has been criticised by the government's education policy, but, ladies and gentlemen, I believe it is always necessary to remain on the ground of reality and the facts in all party-political discussions. I quote from this leaflet "Education for the Future" which was sent by SPÖ for popular desire. In this it is said, and Dr. Gusenbauer says literally: "Separation in the school sector leads to a shortage of educational opportunities and a reduction in the level of schools." Ladies and gentlemen! That's not true. And this is also easy to prove. So much money has never been spent on education, and never has such a wide range of new educational opportunities been created in such a short time. I would also like to show this quite clearly by means of figures. In 2002, the education budget reached the highest proportion of the expenditure of all departments – the budget we have already decided, you can check it – which is 13.7 percent. Every seventh tax schilling is spent on education. In the 2001 budget, 109 billion schillings are available, more than the last government of the old coalition spent in 1999. The schools have no reduced resources for their effort. They have the same resources and get funds from the computer billion. In addition, we have commissioned 1.1 billion Schilling schools. That can be seen! Some say that there is a reduction in the level, but that is not true. Austria, according to an international study, the PIMSS study, is not Europe-wide, according to an international study, for example, in the assessment of subjects in primary schools at the fourth place. Webster University, a very renowned American university, is counting 21 points on an Austrian mechanite when he takes up a degree at Webster University because the level of the Austrian Matura is so high. And the HTL degree is considered by the OECD as a tertiary degree. In order to further improve quality, we use additional resources through the Council for Research and Technology Development for scientific education, we have created 4,000 new jobs for students in the IT field, there are eleven new Backalaureat and Magister studies in the IT field, and there are nearly 1,000 new colleges. These are the facts! Education is therefore at the heart of this government. Ladies and gentlemen! The participation in this popular desire clearly points out: The Austrians know that we have good schools, good teachers and good teachers. And that's why they didn't sign very many. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I would now like to answer the individual questions of this urgent question. Question 1: How to evaluate You want the demands of education? Ladies and gentlemen! Four demands are already fulfilled. Free access to education is guaranteed, the permeability in the education system is given, the social security of students has been improved, a service-oriented service right has been created and there are no reductions in the supply of education. [[Abg. May. Kogler: But not free!]] – The four first claims are fulfilled. The next demands are old hats: the old hat of the entire school, the old hat of the abolition of apprenticeship training and the introduction of a professional school. What you need to talk about is the reduction of the number of school students. But this must also be seen in connection with the declining numbers of students that will come to us in the next few years. On question 2: How high are the Federal Government's investments in education? 110 billion schilling, every seventh schilling is spent on education, including 7 billion schilling for research investment, of which 2.3 billion schilling for basic research. Question 3: What is Austria's position with its educational expenditure? In the OECD we take sixth place with our educational expenditure. In the context of the EU, we take third place. Question 4: How high are the average schooling peaks? Primary school: 19.8, primary school: 22,9, Gymnasium: 24.5, secondary and higher education: 24,3. However, I would like to point out that there are a number of groups in different subjects, that there are the main schools in the service groups and that there are shared in foreign languages in the gymnasium. I would like to point out an interesting phenomenon because it is always said that it is being saved. There are more than 200 constituencies in the gymnasias in Vienna alone with five or less students. Five or less are together in a group. In other words, a lot is spent on education. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Question 5 : the future scenario. – We will have to look very closely at how the number of students is developing. In the schools we have 3 500 students less this year. This will continue to work in the primary schools, in the gymnasias, and then we have to think about what we can do with the maximum number of students on the basis of this development. On question 6: Reduction in the age of schooling: I mean, this should be discussed very carefully. In most countries it is common to send a child six years old to school. And I think children should have a childhood, and we shouldn't take their childhood. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Question 7: What are your priorities for ensuring quality? Many initiatives are being taken. I have mentioned the project "QiS – Quality in Schools" today, and I would like to make one clear: All projects running within the framework of "quality in schools" can be retrieved from our homepage. Since 1999, the projects have been on the Internet, and so far we have had 10.3 million access. That means we're not hiding anything, we're going to give you everything open to the discussion. We are in the development of performance standards, we have the performance-oriented state teacher service law, and there are initiatives to improve mathematical-natural education. Question 8: What are the priorities of information technologies? It was created the computer billions distributed over three years to schools. It's all federal schools networked. 66 percent of compulsory schools are equipped with computers. I would like to thank all the countries and communities in their capacity as school-keepers, who provide the compulsory schools so great! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] On the question 9: Applications for study aid. ==References== On November 28,550 students submitted applications. The deadline is still up to 15. December, and we very much hope that all those who need it will make these applications so that we can grant funding. Question 10: What is the "university billion" used for in 2001 and 2002? 500 million will be given to universities this year, a billion next year for lecture hall and seminar room equipment, improvements in the library area, modernisation of courses, improvement of service, equipment of new courses of study, measures in the foreign language field and other measures on which universities will decide autonomously. [[Abg. Owl: Against one-stopping blankets!]] So I mean, the universities can, with these contributions they receive, improve their courses in such a way that students can complete their studies in the time that is planned. On the last question: How do you assess the drug campaign of socialist youth in schools? My previous speaker quoted the text. I also think that this text is tasteless, I think it is a cultureless text. I would like to point out quite clearly that the principals are obliged not to hang up such letters and leaflets at schools and not to distribute them, and I hope that all the principals in Austria have done so.

For example, after completing my studies – and for a long time working – I still enrolled chemistry in parallel to a doctoral degree in Jus. Then I quit because I thought: If I do the dissertation after all, it is embarrassing to have so many dissertation semesters, so I still write down chemistry, because I certainly do not study that. What's the result? – Of course, it is not particularly useful, because even the chemist is assigned to that money, the students of other fields of study are missing. If my own step nurse, because she actually wants to learn physiotherapy, writes a year of psychology next to work and visits there from time to time some courses that are already overcrowded, then that doesn't make sense. All those who have done so – quite legitimately and rightly – now do not do so and contribute to the money coming where it is actually needed and where people deliberately want to finish their studies. I think that's right and important. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The redistribution we had is now reversed. Now it is the case that the first step was to increase the scholarships by 10 000 s a year, and the second, to increase the number of grant recipients by 12 500. This means that every fourth student in Austria now has the right to study aid. At present, however, not 25 percent, but only 17 percent of students have sought this study aid. The ministry has turned ads on, and we are making a comprehensive effort to find out. And with that I come back to the ÖH and to this educational folk desire. I live around the corner at the University of Innsbruck. I've got air tickets there, and it's been pasted by the ÖH: Go, sign up for the education People's desire! - That was comprehensive! ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e21113	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! Return to the financial statements for the year 2000: The national accounts for 2000 show a deficit of 39.3 billion schilling. That's exactly 1.4 percent of gross domestic product. The Federal Estimate 2000 has also shown a deficit of 54.6 billion schilling. The favourable economic situation has been used. The deficit decreased by 15 billion schilling. No one would have thought so. No one trusted us with that. The re-indebtedness was stopped. We are proud of these figures. The year 2000 was a trend change in Austrian budget policy. For the first time, three budgets were drawn up in one year. They all served, or served, the goal of achieving the zero deficit in 2002. Without administrative reform measures, the zero deficit will already be reached in 2001. No new debt means no new interest and no further burden on the young generation. With an average interest rate of 5.5 percent, this deficit reduction alone means an interest saving of around 840 million schilling per year. Within two years, we have turned a huge debt mountain into a zero deficit. We're not going to make new debts for the first time in 50 years. Public debt in 2000 amounts to a total of 1,782 billion schilling, which is 62.9% of gross domestic product. This means a 1.5 percent reduction compared to 1999. In today's debate, Mr Edlinger said that all this had been done at the expense of the unemployed and pensioners, that it was 45 billion schilling to achieve this zero deficit. I think that if you get 45 billion schilling from the unemployed and the pensioners, then we actually have rich unemployed and rich pensioners. That can't be it, it must have hit all levels of population, and I believe that is the case. Mr Gusenbauer, too, said yesterday that these 45 billion schilling per household mean 15 000 s. I can come back to Mr. Finanzminister Karl-Heinz Grasser, who said that the comparison makes us sure. The comparison makes us really sure! 30 years of Social Democratic Finance Ministers have incurred debts of 2 247 billion schilling. In 1970 we had 43 billion schilling debts. They generate an average of 70 billion schilling debts per year. This increase from 43 billion to 2 247 billion – it is almost impossible to believe – means an increase of over 5,000 percent. If I also calculate the interest burden of 100 billion Schilling and compare it with the 43 billion Schilling and the 15 000 S from Mr Gusenbauer, it is easy to calculate that 100 billion Schilling Interests represent an annual burden of 33 000 S per household. If we didn't have this interest-rate burden, every Austrian would do much better. We will make it possible in the next few years that the Austrians will continue to be so good and much better.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e23165	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! The participants in economic and monetary union are obliged, in accordance with Community legislation, to draw up and implement Stability Pacts, which aim at balanced budgets in the medium term or produce surplus budgets. The previous Stability Pact could no longer meet these conditions, and the stability objectives set in them were insufficient from a European point of view. In June 2001, therefore, the Federal Government, the Länder and the municipalities reached agreement on a renewed Austrian Stability Pact, which provides for a general government budget deficit of minus 1.3 percent and provides for balanced general government budgets from 2002 to 2004. Thanks to the consistent budgetary policy of this Federal Government, the zero deficit will be able to be achieved already this year. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] [[Abg. Haigermoser: Bravo! ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e23430	The next speaker is Mr Müller. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e23451	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! After the agreement on the financial compensation and further detailed negotiations between the financial compensatory partners, it was agreed on 18 and 25 February. In June 2001, the Federal Government reached agreement on the text of a new Austrian Stability Pact. The 20-page pact contains important provisions, some of which I would like to highlight. Increased stability and their contributions and burden sharing: The Austrian Stability Pact established stability contributions for the federal, state and local authorities to ensure that Austria has a balanced general government budget from 2002 onwards. The Federal Government's commitments are to allow a deficit of a maximum of 2.05 percent in 2001 and a maximum of 0.75 percent of GDP in 2002 to 2004. As we have already been informed by our Finance Minister Karl-Heinz Grasser, we will already have a balanced budget in 2001. From 2001 to 2004, countries undertake to jointly contribute an average budget surplus of 0.75% of GDP, but at least 23 billion schilling annually to the general government consolidation path. The largest brokings are on Vienna with around 4.8 billion schillings as well as Lower Austria and Upper Austria each with 4.2 billion schillings. For countries, support is up to 0.15 percent and for municipalities up to 0.10 percent. Payments shall be made in subsequent years in order to ensure that the entire period of validity of the stability contribution is achieved. There is also a common budgetary coordination. For the effective implementation of these commitments, the Federal Government, the Länder and the municipalities shall coordinate their budget management. Political coordination committees are hereby set up. Decisions in these bodies shall be taken by agreement. In addition, the Federal Government, the Länder and the municipalities have to align themselves with the medium-term guidelines in the decision-making process on the annual budget estimates. At the last financial committee meeting, this Stability Pact was approved by both the representatives of the government parties and those of the SPÖ. At this meeting, our Minister of Finance confirmed that it was necessary to have a balanced budget in the economic cycle, which did not exclude deficits in economic times or surpluses in more economic times. It is also fully convinced that the countries will comply with the Stability Pact. As far as economic development is concerned, Austria is still better off than many other countries, such as Germany or Italy, despite revised growth forecasts. This pact is an important milestone on the way to the stability of our finances. I now ask the House to give its approval to this agreement. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e23664	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! The Burgenland celebrates its 80-year membership with Austria this year. The peace agreements following the First World War had brought many hardships and disappointments for Austria. It was only after tough negotiations that the recognition of national self-determination succeeded in ensuring that the Burgenland of the Republic of Austria was granted as an equal federal state. Nevertheless, the Burgenland was not able to be taken over until 1921 after armed resistance from Hungarian Free Schärler with the help of Gendarmerie and the army of Austria. Despite the difficult twenties and thirty years and the terrible time of war and nationalism, the will to build up has prevailed over and over again. Following the restrictions on the time of occupation and the situation on the dead border of the Iron Curtain, the citizens have created a flourishing country, a region with quality of life and excellent prospects for the future. We are also grateful for this anniversary for the benefits and victims of all those people who have given our country the basis for this rise, because these eight decades were a period of hardship, great benefits and joint efforts. We Burgenländer are a sincere, zealous people who like to give themselves. Nevertheless, we are looking forward to the target grant of 55 million Schilling, which we want to decide today: 55 million schilling for education, employment and the economy; 55 million schillings to strengthen cross-border cooperation, to maintain linguistic diversity and to strengthen the identity of the country – well-invested money with added value for the whole of Austria. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I can invite all those who are looking for the special to convince themselves of the uniqueness of the recent federal state of Austria. A short journey through the Burgenian seasons may perhaps make a little sense of more. Ladies and gentlemen! 300 days of sunshine and 2,000 hours of sunshine throughout the year, the possibility of multiple forms of water, as well as the natural conditions, combined with the climatic advantages, give the Burgenland a unique home advantage. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] In March, the first plants already sprout in the Pannonian low plain, while in western Austria the sunskiing is applied. Spring experiences extend over the awakening of nature, the migration of birds in the national park, the tasting of young wine up to the sporty summer opening. [[Abg. Böhacker: How does it look to play with golf?]] The summer is marked by a pulsating cultural run from the renowned Seefestspiele in Mörbisch to the opera festival in the Römersteinbruch St. Margarethen to the numerous castle and castle games. Almost seamless it then goes into the wine-culinary autumn. Dive into the warm, relaxing waves of hot hot temperatures, it is then in cold winter, until the first rays of the sun awaken nature and announce the spring awakening. The international projects National Park Neusiedlersee or the Nature Parks in central and southern Burgenland will also benefit from the jubilee award – interesting projects also with regard to the European eastern enlargement, which will undoubtedly set new standards and create new competitions. But competition revives the senses, and so the Burgenland with its charm and its original naturalness – supported by its jubilee gift – will also be the secret tip in the future. Ladies and gentlemen! I thank you for your approval, and: Welcome to Burgenland!

When we decided on the so-called savings packages, it meant sweat, blood, and tears. This time it is the first tax-amending law that does not remove any additional taxes. It is the first tax-amendment law to bring sensitive administrative simplifications. In this tax-amendment law, the core is the Fees Act, with which we abolish some due acts. For example, the fee is waived in case of proxy, private certificates and protocols. We thus achieve a huge administrative simplification. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Another change concerns the date on which the fee debt was incurred. So far, it has been called upon to pay if one has expressed his concern. In the future, the fee will not be due until the work is done. First the performance and then the payment – a completely new philosophy in public administration! Another important point of this reform in the Fees Act concerns the final abolition of stamps. Ladies and gentlemen, you will, of course, remember that the predecessor of the current Minister for Finance, Rudolf Edlinger, and his then State Secretary Ruttenstorfer have celebrated the abolition of stamps. ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e24656	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! The government template on the Insurance Supervision Act to be adopted today is intended to transpose provisions of the EU Directive 2000 into Austrian law. The implementation of this Directive requires changes to the Insurance Supervision Act, to the Motor Vehicle Liability Insurance Act, to the Motor Vehicles Act and to the Traffic Victim Protection Act. The aim of this draft is to improve the legal status of persons who have become victims of a motor vehicle accident abroad and who have to claim their replacement claims against the foreign liability insurer. The purpose of this new scheme is to require Member States to make up to 20 years of age. January 2002 to set up or recognise compensation points. However, the applicability of this Directive applies only to accidents in countries which have introduced the Green Insurance Card system. In order to improve the service, the following measures are necessary: Insurance undertakings shall be obliged to appoint their own compensation officer for each other EU Member State, and shall also ensure that the injured person can quickly contact the respective liability insurance and the competent compensation officer. Furthermore, the association of insurance undertakings has to keep a register of liability insurance for motor vehicles authorised in the country and to inform the authorised persons of this register. In conclusion, in some areas Austria has already fulfilled its homework long ago. Since 1968, there has been the direct right of action provided for in the EU Directive in the field of motor vehicle liability. Thank you.

We Socialists also believe that the data situation in the education sector in Austria is not particularly good and that we need such data. School site security, school room security, personnel planning, budget planning and various other Austrian specificities require some figures. At international level too, our numerical material is generally not sufficient. For example, in the various international statistics, we do not have data from the pre-school field, we do not have data in the field of adult education, we do not have data in the school equipment. All this is missing in international statistics in the column "Austria". However, we believe that, as this Federal Government deals with education statistics, far beyond what is necessary. In contrast to the previous census – and such data collections are supposed to replace the census in the future – they want to go far beyond the data collected there. While it has been common practice to ask the school career, i.e. folk school, primary school, AHS and the highest education degree, the social security number, the religious profession, the special educational needs and the question of the special or ordinary pupils is now asked about. Even the use of transfer benefits from the family compensation fund is collected. This is not enough, in addition, to the educational career of parents, to question the profession of parents, the position of parents in the profession. I don't think that's useful or necessary. For example, they must note that 800 000 people change their jobs each year in Austria. How do you intend to consider the answer to the question of employment to be relevant after four or five years? Ladies and gentlemen! Modern education statistics do not need personal data, because education planning, management, educational budget issues, student movements or personnel management does not depend on personal data. This is also absolutely untypical at international level! Only in the case of various depth investigations on special questions are solid and personal data collected. However, this is only done in limited sampling studies. Furthermore, we are not sure in this draft law whether data protection is sufficiently guaranteed. Critical comments from the Data Protection Council and the Federal Chancellery, in particular the Constitutional Service, also point out that data protection must be given particular attention. In the draft there are four or five references to regulations to be adopted which we do not know, but precisely these regulations concern the immediate implementation of the law and thus the use of data and data security. Finally, I would like to point out that the SPÖ is of the opinion – because private schools are also involved in a modified form, but one can nevertheless clearly read in the opinions of constitutional experts – that this law absolutely requires a two-thirds majority. They reject it. Because this is extremely difficult, we have proposed to the teaching committee to follow these things in order to secure citizens' rights, to secure constitutional justice, to set up a subcommittee, to invite experts to take some time to adopt an absolutely safe, common law. You don't want to talk to us about such things. Therefore, you cannot expect us to give this law in this form. The other two proposals, which I can say here from this point of view, will be approved. Thank you.

In the meantime, a number of also socio-democratic ministers have been responsible for this and have never changed the exact collection of these data that you criticise here. In part, of course, this educational documentation law, which should also be said, replaces the census. It was consensus here in the house that the way in which the census is carried out is almost felt as a nuisance by the citizens. Today, through electronic data processing, we have the opportunity to simplify things even there. I therefore think that this law is also a law that takes time into account. However, I also believe that, in implementing regulations, we must certainly pay very close attention to what data are actually important for the field of education. So when we talk about transfers, of course we mean those data relating to education, in particular school-free travel and school-booking. Thus, all data will be encrypted, and personal data will not be passed on at all. Only information to simplify administration will be provided. In this context, I would like to ask you to note that all requests made to the Ministry can be answered considerably easier if we have extensive statistics. I would just like to inform you that since 1996, 561 requests have been made to the Ministry of Education and 507 requests to the Ministry of Science, a total of 1068 requests to be answered in detail with all the data. In this respect, this law will bring us a substantial simplification and precisely these extensive statistics.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e27466	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! That this legal regulation is necessary, important and desirable, so that I agree not only with the colleagues of the government groups, but apparently in small parts, also with colleagues of the opposition, who unfortunately cannot go along with it. Why the regulation is necessary, this has now been explained in detail in the contributions; I do not want to repeat that again. Just briefly summarized on the subject of data protection: Proposals of the Data Protection Council are implemented, in which educational evidence provides for non-reciprocal encryption in the form of the educational evidence. The mandate of STATISTIC AUSTRIA, which is subject to special obligations of secrecy, is to take precautions and criminal law is in charge of the prosecution of abuse. Ladies and gentlemen! However, the certain risks that I do not deny are really great, eminent benefits. Data required in the field of education can finally be submitted to one place and accessed from one place. Additional templates may be withdrawn, as well as a further direct survey. Thus, the use of once reported and stored data, as in some other countries already. There is no longer any question of individual areas, data can be obtained through statistical evaluations, without forms and extensive new questionnaires. For planning purposes there are automation-supported evaluations, extensive manual evaluations are missing. In one word: Administrative simplification. For all of us this means that there is complete statistical data, as they are sometimes wanted and requested by us, that is, by Parliament, on educational participation, educational development, student flows – we have already heard all of this – it means access to the basis for medium and long-term analyses, it means optimization of planning through high-quality data that can serve as planning bases. Ladies and gentlemen! The law makes an important contribution to improving the data material required by Parliament and the Court of Auditors. It replaces double and triple surveys with uniform educational standards. It allows optimal use of resources, it replaces paper with modern electronics. As a result, this law makes an important contribution to the efficient handling of taxpayers' money and administrative simplification. ==References====External links== ==References====External links==

Dr. One.]] It is a shame that the opposition, for whatever reason, is against it once again! We support this law. Good luck!
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e28678	No further words are available. In this way, we come to the individual votes, first of all we vote on the draft law concerning a federal law, which amends the Bundesmuseen Act and the Research Organisation Act, the Federal Act establishing a Museum Quarter and Company Society and the Law on the Financing of the Acquisition of the "Collection Leopold". This is the template 850 of the Supplements. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who agree to this bill to give a sign in this regard. – This was decided by a majority in second reading. We shall now proceed to the third reading. I should like to ask that those ladies and gentlemen who also give their agreement at third reading express this. – The proposal was adopted by a majority vote in third reading. We shall vote on the motion for a resolution tabled by the honourable Member. Muttons and comrades on increasing the purchase budget of the Austrian National Library. I would ask those ladies and gentlemen who are in favour of this motion for a resolution tabled by the honourable Member on the subject. – The request has not found a majority and is therefore rejected. Next we come to the vote on the draft federal law on the Austrian Library Association and Service Society with limited liability, government template 830 of the supplements. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who agree to the draft law to give a sign in this regard. – Decision-making shall be adopted unanimously at second reading. We shall now proceed to the third reading. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who also give their approval to the draft law at third reading to give an indication of this. – I notice: This was also unanimously adopted at third reading. 22. Point Report of the Committee on Immunities on the request of the Vienna National Court of Criminal Matters (9aE Vr 5754/01, Hv 3146/01) for approval of the official prosecution of the Deputies to the National Council ing. Peter Westenthaler(841 of the Supplements)
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e28760	We're coming to the 22nd. Agenda item. There is no need for reporting; there is also no mention of a word. We shall therefore proceed to the vote on the request made by the Committee on Immunities in 841 of the supplements, which reads as follows: 1. In response to the request of the Regional Court of Criminal Matters in Vienna of 27 July, the Court of Justice of the European Communities referred to the Court of Justice for a preliminary ruling a question for a preliminary ruling on the interpretation of Council Regulation (EEC) No 1408/71 on the interpretation of Council Regulation (EEC) No 1408/71 on the interpretation of Council Regulation (EEC) No 1408/71 on the application of the laws of the Member States relating to the laws of the Member Peter Westenthaler will be in the sense of Art. 57 para. 3 B-VG notes that a link between the criminal action claimed by the private prosecutors and the political activity of the Deputies to the National Council ing. Peter Westenthalerbestand. 2. An official persecution of the MP to the National Council ing. Peter Westenthaler is approved. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who want to approve these two points of this motion to give a sign on this point. – This is decided by majority vote. The agenda is exhausted. Vote on requests for time-limits
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00084_d7e28820	We shall now proceed to further votes to be taken. First of all, we are voting on the request to the Environment Committee for reporting on Mrs Dr Glawischnig's request 400/A (E) concerning the conversion of the electricity supply of the Federal Buildings to Ecostrom. ==References====External links== I ask those ladies and gentlemen who agree with Mrs Glawischnig's request for a deadline to do so. – This is the minority. The application is therefore rejected. Next, we shall vote on Mrs Glawischnig's request to report to the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection on Amendment No 446/A (E) by Mrs Glawischnig concerning the modification of EU nuclear policy until the 11th time. ==References====External links== A debate has not been requested. We shall therefore proceed to the vote immediately. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who are in favour of this request for a deadline to be approved. – This is the minority. The application is therefore rejected. Inflow
